## 👤 ユーザー　`2026-10-18 00:40:03`

# Modelica言語のIDEを作成する。以下の要件について現在の技術を調査し、プラン構築をせよ。
## 最初のステップとしてOpenModelicaやRumocaを参考にし、どのような機能が必要か整理する
## HTMLスライドとMarkdown解説書をセッションごとに作成
## セッション履歴保存やCLAUDE.md、SKILL.mdなどを作成
- 主要言語: Rust
  - APIを用意: Rust, Gleam, Elixir, Zig, Julia, Python
- デスクトップ環境, ブラウザ環境, CLI, Notebook(marimo)
  - ブラウザではWASMでゲストのリソースを消費する
- OS
  - Debian系, Windows11, Android
- connectからObsidianのようなネットワークグラフを描画
- OSSとして公開することを目標にするため、依存するライブラリのライセンス形態に注意
- 類似プロジェクトとしてRumocaを参考にする
- 遅延評価を用いて処理を軽量化したり、シミュレーション可能なモデルかシミュレーション時にAssert
- ソルバはOpenModelicaやDymolaのように複数対応する
- FMIは3.0&3.0、Co-Simulationなどは必須条件とする

# このリポジトリでの作業ルール

過去問PDFを一次資料として、資格試験の学習教材（理論書・解説HTML・Ankiデッキ）を
生成・検証するリポジトリ。全体像は [README.md](README.md)。

以下は**このリポジトリで作業するとき必ず守ること**。

---

## 1. 作業は git worktree の feature ブランチで行う

`main` の作業ツリーを直接編集しない。作業ごとに worktree を切る。

```bash
cd ~/monorepo/Chaos/20260816_exam
git fetch --all --prune 2>/dev/null || true
git worktree add -b feature/<topic> .git-worktree/<topic> main
cd .git-worktree/<topic>
uv sync                      # ← 必須。理由は下記
```

- ブランチ名は `feature/<topic>`（例 `feature/add-denki-1kyu`）。`<topic>` は英小文字とハイフン。
- worktree は `.git-worktree/<topic>`（リポジトリ内・`.gitignore` 済みなので追跡されない）。
- 作業が終わって取り込んだら片付ける:
  `git worktree remove .git-worktree/<topic> && git branch -d feature/<topic>`

**`.venv` を worktree 間で共有・symlink してはいけない。**
`examkit` は editable install（`.venv/
… （残り 4,118 字は生ログを参照）
```

<result># Research report: FMI, Rust FMI crates, solvers and incremental infrastructure for a Rust Modelica IDE (as of 2026-10-18)

Data comes from crates.io API records, GitHub READMEs and license files, and fmi-standard.org / ssp-standard.org. **[U]** marks something I could not confirm. The FMI 2 vs 3 details come from my knowledge of the spec, not from a page I fetched.

## 1. FMI and SSP

- **Current version: FMI 3.0.2**, released 2024-11-27 together with 2.0.5. Both are bugfix-only releases, and 3.0.2 FMUs are interchangeable with 3.0 and 3.0.1. I found no 3.0.3 or 3.1 **[U]**. https://fmi-standard.org/news/2024-11-27-fmi-2.0.5-and-3.0.2
- **What FMI 3.0 adds over 2.0** (2.0 has only Model Exchange and Co-Simulation):
  - A third interface, Scheduled Execution.
  - Clocks: time-based and triggered, with clocked variables.
  - Co-Simulation gets event mode, an intermediate-update callback (outputs between communication points) and early return from `doStep`.
  - Multi-dimensional arrays with structural parameters.
  - New types: Float32/64, Int8 to UInt64, Binary and Clock.
  - `terminalsAndIcons.xml`, `buildDescription.xml` for source FMUs, and layered standards stored under `extra/`.
  - In Model Exchange, `fmi3UpdateDiscreteStates` replaces `fmi2NewDiscreteStates`.
  - Spec: https://fmi-standard.org/docs/3.0/
- **Layered standards** (https://fmi-standard.org/news/):

| Standard | Status |
|---|---|
| LS-XCP | 1.0.0, released 2024-12-03 |
| LS-BUS | 1.0 released 2025-07-21; 1.3.0-beta.1 on 2026-06-25 (LIN and Ethernet in beta) |
| LS-STRUCT | 1.0.0-beta.1 on 2026-04-28 (sampled maps). The homepage says "rc.1", which conflicts **[U]** |
| LS-REF | 1.0.0-beta.1, 2026-07 |
| **LS-DAE** | **1.0.0-alpha.1, 2026-09-11.** Worth watching closely for Modelica export |

- **SSP:** 1.0 (2019-03-05), 1.0.1 (2022), **2.0 (2024-12-20)**, **2.0.1 (2026-05-05)**, plus SSP-LS-Traceability 1.0.0 (2025-08). Schemas are BSD-2-Clause and the documentation is CC-BY-SA-4.0. https://ssp-standard.org/news/ , https://github.com/modelica/ssp-standard
- **Licenses:**
  - fmi-standard: spec text is CC-BY-SA-4.0; C headers and XSD files are **BSD-2-Clause**. https://github.com/modelica/fmi-standard/blob/main/LICENSE.txt
  - Reference-FMUs: **BSD-2-Clause**, FMI 2 and 3. Models: BouncingBall, Dahlquist, Feedthrough, Resource, Stair, StateSpace, VanDerPol and Clocks; the README also lists Roberts (DAE). https://github.com/modelica/Reference-FMUs

## 2. Rust FMI crates

All of these live in https://github.com/jondo2010/rust-fmi and are licensed **MIT OR Apache-2.0**. The project is small (41 stars) and pre-1.0.

- **`fmi` 0.8.0** (2026-07-28): imports FMI 2.0 and 3.0 FMUs. Companion crates are `fmi-sys` and `fmi-schema` (XML), plus `fmi-test-data` and `fmi-ls-bus`.
- **`fmi-sim` 0.7.0:** "pure Rust FMI simulator". The README calls it work in progress.
- **`fmi-export` 0.3.0** (first published 2026-02-01): **this is the export path you asked about, but it covers FMI 3.0 only.**
  - You write a `cdylib`, add `#[derive(FmuModel)]`, and call `export_fmu!`.
  - Model Exchange is on by default; Co-Simulation and Scheduled Execution can be switched on with flags.
  - It supports event indicators and clocks.
  - `cargo-fmi` generates `modelDescription.xml` and zips the `.fmu`.
  - An optional `rumoca` feature turns `.mo` files into Rust.
  - Docs: https://docs.rs/fmi-export , https://docs.rs/cargo-fmi
- I found no separate published crate named "fmi-rs" or "rust-fmi" **[U]**. I found nothing that exports FMI 2.
- **Reference implementations in other languages:**
  - FMPy: **BSD-2-Clause**; it bundles SUNDIALS (BSD-3-Clause). https://github.com/CATIA-Systems/FMPy
  - FMI Library (FMIL): **BSD-3-Clause**. https://github.com/modelon-community/fmi-library
  - fmusdk: BSD-2-Clause, archived in 2021. https://github.com/qtronic/fmusdk
- Related: `rumoca` 0.7.28, a Modelica compiler in Rust, Apache-2.0. https://github.com/cognipilot/rumoca

## 3. Numerical solvers

- **diffsol**: BDF (NDF variant), SDIRK/ESDIRK (TR-BDF2, ESDIRK34), Tsit45. It handles semi-explicit DAEs through a mass matrix, root-finding events, and forward and adjoint sensitivities. Linear algebra is nalgebra or faer, with optional KLU.
  - Its JIT, **diffsl** (MIT), uses Cranelift by default and LLVM optionally. The two READMEs disagree on which LLVM versions are supported (15–18 vs 15–20) and on whether Enzyme is needed **[U]**.
  - https://github.com/martinjrobins/diffsol , https://github.com/martinjrobins/diffsl
- **SuiteSparse licenses** (https://github.com/DrTimothyAldenDavis/SuiteSparse/blob/dev/LICENSE.txt):
  - **KLU, BTF, LDL and CSparse/CXSparse are LGPL-2.1-or-later.**
  - AMD, CAMD, COLAMD and CCOLAMD are BSD-3-Clause.
  - **UMFPACK, SPQR and CHOLMOD Supernodal/Modify/MatrixOps are GPL-2.0-or-later.**
  - The CHOLMOD Core license text was blank in the copy I read **[U]**.
- **Main license risk:** `russell_sparse` always builds and links **UMFPACK, which is GPL-2.0-or-later**. If you use `russell_ode`'s Radau5, avoid that dependency.
- **symbolica:** licensed under the "Symbolica Source-Available License 1.0". It is **not OSI open source**: running it needs a runtime license, modified copies can't be redistributed, and there are clauses against competing products and AI training. **Avoid it.** `egg` (MIT) is the safe option. https://github.com/benruijl/symbolica/blob/master/License.md

## 4. Incremental computation, parsing, LSP and JIT

- **salsa:** used by rust-analyzer and ty. ty's browser playground is built on it, which suggests it works on wasm32. An older note said wasm32 lacks `catch_unwind`, which salsa uses for cycle handling; that note is struck through, so this is probably fixed **[U]**.
- **tree-sitter:** the `wasm` feature means loading grammars *through wasmtime*, not running in a browser. Building for wasm32-unknown-unknown needs a libc/sysroot shim or the c2rust fork; in the browser, use web-tree-sitter.
- **tree-sitter-modelica** (OpenModelica): licensed **AGPL-3.0 or OSMC-PL 1.8**. I couldn't tell whether that means -only or -or-later **[U]**. It targets Modelica 3.5 and is immature (39 commits). **Copyleft: I'd write your own rowan-based parser.** https://github.com/OpenModelica/tree-sitter-modelica
- **tower-lsp** has had no release since 2023-08. Use **tower-lsp-server** (community fork) or rust-analyzer's **lsp-server**.
- **Cranelift JIT** can't execute in a browser because a page can't map executable memory. In WASM, generate wasm modules instead **[U: whether Cranelift codegen itself builds for wasm32]**.

## Summary table

| crate | purpose | license (SPDX) | WASM ok? | maturity |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| fmi / fmi-sim / fmi-export | FMI import / sim / FMI 3 export | MIT OR Apache-2.0 | [U] (loads native libs) | 0.8 / 0.7 / 0.3, young |
| diffsol | ODE/DAE (BDF, SDIRK, ERK, events) | MIT | likely without JIT/KLU [U] | 0.17.1 (2026-09-28), active |
| diffsl | ODE DSL JIT | MIT | no (JIT) | small |
| sundials-sys / sundials | SUNDIALS bindings (CVODE/IDA) | BSD-3-Clause | no (emscripten only) | 0.6.2 (2024-12) / 0.4.1 |
| russell_ode | Radau5, DoPri5/8 | MIT (links GPL UMFPACK) | no | 3.3.1, active |
| ode_solvers | explicit RK only, no events | Apache-2.0 (older: BSD-3-Clause) | yes | 0.6.2, mature |
| differential-equations | ODE/DAE/DDE/SDE, events | Apache-2.0 | likely [U] | 0.7.0 (2026-10-11) |
| peroxide | sci-comp; RK + GL4 (no BDF) | MIT OR Apache-2.0 | yes (pure-Rust default) | 0.43.1 |
| faer | dense + sparse LA (pure Rust) | MIT | yes | 0.24.4, mature |
| nalgebra | dense LA | Apache-2.0 (older: BSD-3-Clause) | yes | 0.35.0 |
| ndarray | n-d arrays | MIT OR Apache-2.0 | yes | 0.17.2 |
| KLU (SuiteSparse) | sparse LU | LGPL-2.1-or-later | hard | mature C |
| symbolica | CAS | source-available (not OSI) | n/a | avoid |
| egg | e-graphs | MIT | yes | 0.11.0 |
| salsa | incremental queries | Apache-2.0 OR MIT | yes (ty playground) [U threads] | 0.28.5 |
| rowan | lossless CST | MIT OR Apache-2.0 | yes | 0.17.0 |
| tree-sitter | incremental parser (C core) | MIT | with shim / web-tree-sitter | 0.27.1 |
| tree-sitter-modelica | Modelica grammar | AGPL-3.0 OR OSMC-PL-1.8 | as tree-sitter | immature |
| tower-lsp | LSP framework | MIT OR Apache-2.0 | no | stale (0.20, 2023) |
| tower-lsp-server | maintained fork | MIT OR Apache-2.0 | [U] | 0.23 |
| lsp-server | rust-analyzer LSP transport | MIT OR Apache-2.0 | no (stdio/threads) [U] | 0.10.0 |
| cranelift-codegen | JIT/AOT codegen | Apache-2.0 WITH LLVM-exception | JIT no | 0.136.3 |
| inkwell | LLVM bindings (LLVM 12–22) | Apache-2.0 | no | 0.10.0 |

**Main takeaways:** keep the core MIT/Apache by building on faer, diffsol, salsa, rowan and egg. Isolate anything LGPL (KLU) or GPL (UMFPACK) behind optional features. Avoid symbolica and tree-sitter-modelica because of their licenses.</result>

## 🤖 Claude　`2026-10-18 00:46:10`

FMI／ソルバ系の調査が完了しました。要点:

- 最新は **FMI 3.0.2**。FMI 3.0 で Scheduled Execution とクロック機構が加わり、FMI 2.0 は ME/CS の2方式のみ。
- Rust の `fmi` は 0.8 で 2.0/3.0 の Import に対応。一方 `fmi-export` は **3.0 専用**なので、FMI 2.0 の Export は自前で実装する必要があります。
- ソルバは diffsol（MIT、BDF/ESDIRK、イベント対応）と faer を基盤にするのが安全です。
- 避けるもの:
  - KLU（LGPL）と UMFPACK（GPL）は optional feature に隔離する。
  - symbolica は OSI 承認の OSS ではない。
  - tree-sitter-modelica は AGPL。

残り2本（Rumoca/OpenModelica、UI/バインディング）の結果を待っています。

<result>I couldn't confirm several things. Each one is marked **(unverified)** below. This isn't legal advice.

## 1. App shells

- **Tauri 2**: `Apache-2.0 OR MIT`. The newest release I saw was v2.11.5 (2026-07-01). It runs on Win10+, Linux (webkit2gtk-4.1), Android 7+ and iOS. It has no browser target of its own, so you ship the same frontend as a static site. Some official plugins still don't support mobile. [repo](https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri), [releases](https://v2.tauri.app/release/tauri/)
- **Dioxus 0.7**: `MIT OR Apache-2.0`, released 2025-09-08, with 0.8-alpha out. It renders to the DOM on the web and through a webview on desktop, Android and iOS. Its native WGPU renderer (Blitz) and Rust hot-patching are experimental. [0.7 post](https://dioxuslabs.com/blog/release-070)
- **Leptos**: `MIT`. Web only. Desktop and Android need Tauri, and Tauri's guide is written for Leptos 0.6. [guide](https://v2.tauri.app/start/frontend/leptos/)
- **egui/eframe**: `MIT OR Apache-2.0`. Runs on web, Windows, Linux, macOS and Android. iOS isn't listed, and the API still breaks between releases. Its text widgets are too weak for a full code editor. [repo](https://github.com/emilk/egui)
- **Slint**: `GPL-3.0-only OR LicenseRef-Slint-Royalty-free-2.0 OR LicenseRef-Slint-Software-3.0`. Android works since 1.5, Rust only. Web builds are for demos only.
  - **OSS issue:** taking the GPL option makes your shipped binaries GPL.
  - The royalty-free option isn't an OSI license, requires attribution and excludes embedded use. That's likely a problem for Debian main **(unverified)**.
  - [license](https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/gh/slint-ui/slint@1.16.0/LICENSE.md), [Android](https://slint.dev/blog/slint-1.5-released)
- **Iced**: `MIT`, still experimental. Desktop and web; no official Android **(unverified)**. [repo](https://github.com/iced-rs/iced)
- **Makepad**: `MIT`. Runs on wasm/WebGL, Windows, Linux, macOS, Android and iOS. Small ecosystem, and you can't embed Monaco or CodeMirror. [repo](https://github.com/makepad/makepad)

**One codebase for desktop, web and Android:** Dioxus, Tauri with any web frontend, egui, or Makepad. Slint is weak on web.

**Code editors:**
- **CodeMirror 6** (`MIT`) works well on mobile. `@codemirror/lsp-client` (`MIT`) talks to a server through a Transport object you write. That transport can post messages to a Web Worker running a language server compiled to wasm. [announcement](https://discuss.codemirror.net/t/codemirror-lsp-client/9309), [worker demo](https://discuss.codemirror.net/t/show-and-tell-codemirror-lsp-client-with-patched-ts-language-server-running-in-web-workers/9337)
- **Monaco** (`MIT`) has more features, but I believe it isn't supported in mobile browsers **(unverified)**. `monaco-languageclient` (`MIT`) supports language servers running in a worker. [repo](https://github.com/TypeFox/monaco-languageclient)

## 2. Graph rendering

- **sigma.js + graphology** (`MIT`): WebGL rendering, with a ForceAtlas2 layout package for graphology. This is the closest to Obsidian's graph view. [repo](https://github.com/jacomyal/sigma.js)
- **cosmos.gl** (`MIT`): runs the force simulation on the GPU in WebGL2 and handles hundreds of thousands of nodes. It's an OpenJS Foundation incubating project.
  - **Don't confuse it with Cosmograph.** The Cosmograph app and library are CC BY-NC 4.0 or commercial.
  - [repo](https://github.com/cosmosgl/graph), [OpenJS](https://openjsf.org/blog/introducing-cosmos-gl), [Cosmograph licensing](https://cosmograph.app/licensing/)
- **force-graph / 3d-force-graph** (`MIT`): canvas (or three.js for 3D), using d3-force for the physics. Examples go up to about 75k elements. The fastest way to prototype. [repo](https://github.com/vasturiano/force-graph)
- **d3-force** (`ISC`) and **cytoscape.js** (`MIT`): not checked this session.
- **Rust-native:**
  - **egui_graphs** (`MIT`): built on petgraph, runs in wasm. Its force layout is a simple O(n²) Fruchterman–Reingold, and the repo says it's "not in active development". [repo](https://github.com/blitzarx1/egui_graphs)
  - **petgraph**: `MIT OR Apache-2.0`.
  - **fdg-sim**: `MIT` from 0.6/0.9.x on, but 0.4.x and the sibling crates `fdg-img`/`fdg-macroquad` are `GPL-3.0`. Pin versions carefully. [docs.rs](https://docs.rs/crate/fdg-sim/latest)
  - **layout-rs** (`MIT`): Graphviz-style layered layout, v0.1.3. [repo](https://github.com/nadavrot/layout)
- **Schematic editors:**
  - **React Flow / Svelte Flow** (xyflow): `MIT`.
  - **maxGraph**: `Apache-2.0`, the successor to mxGraph, under active development. [repo](https://github.com/maxGraph/maxGraph)
  - **JointJS core**: `MPL-2.0`; JointJS+ is commercial. [repo](https://github.com/clientIO/joint)
  - **ELK.js**: `EPL-2.0`. That's file-level copyleft, so using it unmodified in an MIT/Apache app is fine. It is GPL-incompatible unless a secondary license is added. Eclipse has a "2026.07 restructuring review" proposing GPLv3 as that secondary license; I couldn't find the outcome. [review](https://gitlab.eclipse.org/eclipsefdn/emo-team/emo/-/work_items/1257), [EPL FAQ](https://Eclipse.org/legal/epl-2.0/faq.php)

## 3. Language bindings

Suggested layering:
```
modelica-core (pure Rust: parse → flatten → DAE → solve; no I/O)
 ├─ modelica-capi (extern "C", opaque handles, JSON/bytes; cbindgen header)
 │    ├─ Zig: @cImport the header
 │    └─ Julia: ccall + BinaryBuilder JLL  (or jlrs julia_module, MIT)
 ├─ modelica-py   (PyO3 directly; maturin)
 ├─ modelica-nif  (Rustler directly) → Elixir; Gleam via @external to the Erlang module
 ├─ modelica-wasm (wasm-bindgen) → browser worker, Gleam JS target, anywidget
 └─ modelica-ffi-kt (uniffi) → Kotlin/Android (unneeded if the UI is Tauri)
```

- **PyO3** is `Apache-2.0 OR MIT` and **maturin** is `MIT OR Apache-2.0`. [PyO3](https://github.com/PyO3/pyo3)
- **marimo** (`Apache-2.0`) WASM notebooks run on Pyodide, cap memory at 2 GB and only emulate threads. [guide](https://docs.marimo.io/guides/wasm/) A Rust module can get in two ways:
  - **Pyodide wheel:** build PyO3 as a `pyemscripten_2026_0` wheel and install it from a URL with micropip. This needs Rust nightly, the exact emsdk version Pyodide uses (about 5.0.3), and `-Z emscripten-wasm-eh`. [Willison](https://simonwillison.net/2026/Feb/6/pyo3-pyodide-wasm/), [pydantic](https://pydantic.dev/articles/emscripten-wheels-pydantic)
  - **anywidget** (`MIT`): its ESM code loads a wasm-bindgen module on the JavaScript side, independent of Pyodide. anywidgets render in static HTML exports since marimo 0.19.10. Whether they work in `html-wasm` exports is **unverified**. [docs](https://docs.marimo.io/api/inputs/anywidget/), [release note](https://newreleases.io/project/pypi/marimo/release/0.19.10)
- **Elixir:** Rustler is `Apache-2.0 OR MIT` and supports the newest three OTP versions. `rustler_precompiled` is `Apache-2.0`. [rustler](https://github.com/rusterlium/rustler), [hex](https://readme.hex.pm/rustler_precompiled/0.8.3)
- **Gleam:** externals only target Erlang and JavaScript. Call the Rustler NIF module from Gleam, or use a wasm-bindgen package on the JS target. [externals](https://gleam.run/documentation/externals/)
- **Zig:** the cbindgen tool is `MPL-2.0`. The generated header comes from your own code, and its `header=` setting lets you stamp your own license on it, which is how Apache OpenDAL does it. That the header isn't MPL-covered is the common reading **(unverified)**.
- **Julia:** jlrs is `MIT`, supports Julia 1.10 to 1.13, and its `julia_module` libraries can ship as BinaryBuilder JLLs. Plain `ccall` on the C ABI is simpler and more stable. [repo](https://github.com/Taaitaaiger/jlrs)
- **uniffi** is `MPL-2.0` and covers Kotlin, Swift and Python (Ruby too), with third-party wasm bindings. **wasm-bindgen** is `MIT OR Apache-2.0`. [uniffi](https://github.com/mozilla/uniffi-rs)

## 4. Licensing

- **Project license:** `MIT OR Apache-2.0`. It matches the Rust ecosystem, Apache adds a patent grant, and it's compatible with SUNDIALS (`BSD-3-Clause`), Monaco/CodeMirror (`MIT`), maxGraph and rumoca (`Apache-2.0`), and MPL-2.0 dependencies as long as those files stay MPL.
- **OpenModelica:** offered under AGPL-3.0 or OSMC-PL 1.8, so don't link or vendor it. If you use it at all, run `omc` as a separate process. Its runtime adds a BSD option. [OMJulia](https://github.com/OpenModelica/OMJulia.jl)
- **Qt:** LGPL. Unnecessary if you use Tauri, so skip it.
- **cargo-deny** (`Apache-2.0 OR MIT`): allow MIT, Apache-2.0, BSD-2/3-Clause, ISC, Zlib, MPL-2.0 and Unicode-3.0; deny GPL/AGPL/OSMC/NC licenses. JS dependencies need a separate license check. [repo](https://github.com/EmbarkStudios/cargo-deny)

## 5. Existing efforts

- **Rumoca** (`Apache-2.0`, CogniPilot Foundation) is the most relevant. It's a Rust Modelica compiler producing a DAE IR, with formatter, linter, LSP and a VS Code extension. It runs in the browser as wasm with workers, installs with pip, and targets CasADi, JAX and Julia. Latest crate I saw is 0.7.28; arXiv paper June 2026. **Consider building on it instead of writing your own core.** [docs.rs](https://docs.rs/crate/rumoca/latest), [paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14998)
- **modelica-language-server** (OSMC-PL 1.8, experimental, v0.3.6) is built on tree-sitter-modelica, which is also under the OSMC license. Don't vendor either one. [repo](https://github.com/OpenModelica/modelica-language-server)
- **Other Rust parsers:** moparse (`MPL-2.0`, work in progress) and modelica-rs (stale ANTLR crate, last updated 2023). [modelica-rs](https://docs.rs/modelica-rs)
- **Dyad** (JuliaHub) is source-available under a modified PolyForm Strict license, free only for non-commercial use, so it isn't OSS. It compiles down to ModelingToolkit.jl. [blog](https://juliahub.com/blog/why-dyad-a-perspective-for-modelica-users)
- **ModelingToolkit.jl and Modia.jl:** `MIT` **(unverified)**. **OMJulia:** BSD-3-Clause, GPL-3.0 or OSMC-PL 1.2, recipient's choice.
- **In-browser work:**
  - Modiator (Elmqvist/Otter): SUNDIALS compiled to wasm. [paper](https://ecp.ep.liu.se/index.php/modelica/article/view/1307)
  - Tiller's Modelica playground. [paper](https://ecp.ep.liu.se/index.php/modelica/article/view/628)
  - Bodylight.js: compiles FMUs to wasm.
  - I found no code license for any of the three.
- **Proprietary:** Modelon Impact (runs in the browser), Wolfram System Modeler, MapleSim. `pymodelica` belongs to the old JModelica.org stack, whose open development ended around 2019 **(unverified)**.

## Recommendation

| Concern | Pick | License |
|---|---|---|
| Shell | Tauri 2 (desktop and Android) plus the same frontend as a static web app; Dioxus 0.7 if you want all-Rust | Apache-2.0 OR MIT |
| Core in the browser | wasm-bindgen module in a Web Worker | MIT OR Apache-2.0 |
| Editor | CodeMirror 6 plus `@codemirror/lsp-client`, LSP in a worker | MIT |
| Compiler/LSP | Fork of or contributions to Rumoca | Apache-2.0 |
| Connect graph | sigma.js + graphology; cosmos.gl for huge graphs | MIT |
| Schematic | maxGraph or xyflow; ELK only if EPL is acceptable | Apache-2.0 / MIT |
| Solver | SUNDIALS (C ABI, wasm-capable) | BSD-3-Clause |
| Bindings | PyO3, Rustler, C ABI for Zig/Julia, uniffi only if needed | Permissive / MPL |
| marimo | anywidget plus wasm-bindgen; Pyodide wheel as a fallback | MIT |
| Audit | cargo-deny | Apache-2.0 OR MIT |
| Avoid | Linking OpenModelica or its tree-sitter grammar, Slint GPL, Cosmograph | AGPL / OSMC / NC |</result>

## 1. Rumoca facts
- **Repo:** github.com/CogniPilot/rumoca. jgoppert is the main committer and there is no rumoca-lang org. Apache-2.0. 140★, last push 2026-10-17, with about 15 commits on Oct 8–16 alone, so it is very active.
- **Version:** v0.10.2 is the latest GitHub release (2026-10-04), and the workspace is already at 0.10.3. crates.io stops at **0.7.28 (2025-12-27)**. From 0.8 on it ships only through GitHub Releases: Python wheels plus `rumoca`/`rumoca-lsp` binaries. Builds need **nightly** Rust (edition 2024).
- **Workspace:** about 57 crates in a phase/IR split.
  - IRs: `ir-ast → ir-flat → ir-dae → ir-solve`, plus `ir-galec`.
  - Phases: `phase-parse/resolve/typecheck/instantiate/flatten/dae/structural/solve/autodiff/codegen`.
  - Simulation and execution: `sim`, `solver-diffsol`, `solver-rk45`, `exec-cranelift/mlir/wasm`.
  - Bindings: `bind-python` (pyo3 0.22), `bind-wasm`.
  - Tools: `tool-lsp` (tower-lsp 0.20), `tool-fmt`, `tool-lint`.
  - Live sessions: `transport-udp/websocket/zenoh` and `input-gamepad/keyboard`.
  - Tests: `test-msl`, `contracts` (spec tests).
- **Parser:** parol =4.2.2 with scnr2. Docs call it error-tolerant enough to run on every keystroke. **[?]** I couldn't confirm whether the grammar is LL(k) or LALR(1), and I found no salsa-style incremental engine.
- **Pipeline:** parse → resolve (stable `DefId`s) → typecheck → instantiate (modifiers, extends, **redeclarations**, conditional components) → flatten → DAE → structural analysis (matching, BLT/Tarjan, Pantelides, tearing, init planning) → Solve IR.
- **Codegen:** MiniJinja templates. Built-in targets: base-modelica, flat-modelica, dae-modelica, c-ode, casadi-ode, jax-ode, cuda-ode, wgsl-ode, rust-ode, rust-fixed-ode, mlir, fmi2, fmi3, fmi-ls-wasm, galec, galec-production, embedded-c-galec. You can add custom targets with `target.toml` + Jinja. Julia and ONNX are also mentioned, and there are recent FMI export/co-sim fixes.
- **Simulation:** yes, it has its own. Solver choices are `auto`, `bdf` (vendored diffsol) and `rk-like`. It does zero-crossing detection, schedules `sample` exactly, uses AD Jacobians, and does sensitivities/adjoints and A/B/C/D linearization. It can stall on event-heavy models.
- **Tooling:** "Rumoca Modelica" VS Code extension with bundled LSP (diagnostics, completion, go-to-definition, references, rename, hover, inlay hints, call hierarchy). There is also a WASM/Monaco playground and `pip install rumoca`.
- **Coverage (MSL 4.1.0 examples, 2026-06-05):**

  | Stage | Models |
  |---|---|
  | Parse | 566 |
  | Flatten | 562 |
  | DAE | 477 |
  | Balanced | 464 |
  | Init solve OK | 224 |
  | Simulate OK | 153 |
  | High agreement with OMC | 89 of 143 compared |

  - Supported: extends, modifiers, connect/flow, arrays, functions, and when/pre/edge/reinit/sample.
  - In v0.7.28, stream, expandable and overconstrained connectors, operator overloading and state machines were not implemented. **[?]** I couldn't confirm their status today. Fluid work was still in progress in v0.10.1.
- **As a dependency:** the license is fine, but the API is unstable, it needs nightly Rust, and crates.io is stale. Use a git dependency pinned to a tag. It is also worth reading as a reference for its IR layering and the LSP/WASM setup.

## 2. OpenModelica facts
- **Releases:** 1.27.1 is the latest (2026-09-08), 1.27.0 came out 2026-07-02/07, and 1.26.0 on 2025-12-18. 1.28.0-dev docs are online.
- **Components:**
  - OMC compiler, written in MetaModelica. The New Frontend (NF) is the default, and the New Backend (NB) passes about 30% of the library tests (2026 workshop).
  - OMEdit (GUI) and OMSimulator (FMI/SSP co-simulation, being redesigned with Python bindings; **[?]** FMI 3 status is partial).
  - OMPython, OMJulia, OMMatlab, OMShell, OMNotebook and OMWebbook. I found no deprecation notice for OMShell or OMNotebook **[?]**.
  - Base Modelica output via a `--baseModelica`-style flag **[?]** for the exact spelling.
- **OMEdit features:**
  - Icon, Diagram, Text and Documentation views (WYSIWYG documentation editor).
  - Libraries, Variables and Messages browsers; drawing connections and shapes.
  - Check Model / Check All, Instantiate, Simulation Setup (flags saved to annotations), re-simulate without recompiling.
  - Plots: time, parametric and array.
  - Algorithmic, transformational and state-machine debuggers; 3D animation.
  - Package manager: install, upgrade, index, conversion scripts.
  - FMU import and export (FMI 1/2/3, ME/CS), SSP editor, data reconciliation, sensitivity/optimization.
  - Encrypted package export, and LSP-style hover and go-to-definition.
- **Pipeline:** parse → NF instantiate/flatten (connection sets, redeclare, evaluate structural parameters) → symbolic simplification → matching (PFPlusExt) → index reduction (Pantelides + dynamic state selection or dummy derivatives) → BLT/Tarjan → tearing (omcTearing/cellier) → C/C++ code generation → runtime with event handling, init and linear/nonlinear solvers. **[?]** The method defaults are from memory; confirm with `omc --help`.

## 3. Solver lists
- **OpenModelica (`-s`):** dassl (default), ida, cvode, euler, heun, rungekutta (deprecated, use gbode), impeuler, trapezoid, imprungekutta, irksco, rungekuttaSsc, symSolver, symSolverSsc, qss, optimization.
  - gbode is a generic RK framework, ESDIRK4 by default, with SDIRK/ESDIRK/Gauss/Radau/Lobatto/Fehlberg/DOPRI45/Merson schemes and bi-rate mode.
  - **[?]** The docs page I fetched only showed dassl, ida, cvode, gbode, euler, rungekutta, symSolver(Ssc) and qss. heun, impeuler, trapezoid, imprungekutta, irksco and rungekuttaSsc are from older docs and memory.
- **Dymola (Dymosim):**
  - Variable step: Lsodar, Dassl, Radau IIa, Esdirk23a/34a/45a, Dopri45, Dopri853, Sdirk34hw, Cerk23/34/45, Cvode.
  - Fixed step: Euler, Rkfix2/3/4.

## 4. Licenses and standards
- **OMC:** OSMC-PL **1.8**. Anyone may choose **AGPL-3.0** or OSMC-PL. The EPL-1.0-based "Internal/External" modes are for OSMC Level 1/2 members only. The old GPL-3 wording is obsolete. **Runtime files are BSD-New / AGPL-3 / OSMC-PL**, so generated simulation code is permissive.
- **What that means for you (not legal advice):**
  - Reading docs or source to learn behaviour and re-implementing it is fine.
  - Running `omc` as a separate process (scripting API, ZMQ, or the CLI over pipes) generally counts as aggregation and is common practice.
  - Statically linking or embedding OMC code puts your binary under AGPL-3 obligations.
  - Copying code requires AGPL. Rumoca's Apache-2.0 is compatible one-way into AGPL/GPLv3.
- **MSL:** 4.1.0 (2025-05-23) is still the latest, BSD-3-Clause.
- **MLS:** **3.7 released (May 2026)**; master is 3.8-dev. I found no evidence of a "Modelica 4".
- **Base Modelica (MCP-0031):** still "In Development" (0.1 roadmap), with experimental export in OMC and a Rumoca template. **[?]** The page I fetched may be stale.
- **eFMI:** 1.0.0 **Beta 2** (candidate draft, 2026-10-07).
- **SSP 2.0:** released Dec 2024/Jan 2025.
- **"Modelica Base Library (MBL)" and "LinkModelica":** **no evidence found.** They may not exist, so drop them unless you have a primary source.

## 5. IDE feature checklist (from both)
1. Error-tolerant parser plus LSP: diagnostics, completion, go-to-definition, references, rename, hover, outline, formatter, linter.
2. Library and package browser, MSL loading, package manager with version/uses annotations.
3. Icon and Diagram views from annotations (Placement, Line, connect routing), editing in both directions with the text.
4. Documentation view (HTML annotation).
5. Parameter dialog (modifiers, replaceable/redeclare choices).
6. Check Model (balance count), Instantiate/flatten view.
7. Simulation setup (experiment annotation, solver choice), run, re-simulate with new parameters.
8. Result plotting (time, parametric, large files), variable browser.
9. Transformational debugger (BLT, index reduction, tearing), algorithmic debugger.
10. FMU export and import, SSP.
11. Codegen targets (Base Modelica, CasADi/JAX, C).
12. WASM and web build, Python API.
13. Running tests against OMC reference trajectories.

# GlobeMode: target architecture and phased roadmap (v0, 2026-10-18)

## 0. What the repo check found
- **The Python package can't be imported.** A Python package name can't start with a digit. The generated `.venv/bin/20261018-globemode` runs `from 20261018_globemode import main`, which is a SyntaxError. Rename the package to `globe_mode`.
- **Only two source files are tracked:** `src/main.rs` and `src/20261018_globemode/__init__.py` (commit `fba8059`, remote `asari300/20261018_GlobeMode`).
  - The `src/{Elixir,Gleam,Julia,Modelica,Python/{marimo,mod,streamlit},Rust/mod}` directories are empty and untracked. They get replaced in P1.
  - `streamlit` isn't one of your targets. Keep it or drop it?
- **`.gitignore` is missing entries.** It has no `.git-worktree/` and no `.claude/` local-state block (the sibling repo has one).
- **The host only has stable 1.99.1 for x86_64.** No wasm or Android targets are installed, and there is no node or pnpm.

## 1. Workspace layout
The root becomes a virtual workspace (`resolver = "3"`, edition 2024, `rust-version = "1.99"`). Top-level directories: `crates/`, `apps/`, `bindings/`, `notebooks/`, `xtask/`.

| Crate | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| `globe-mode-core` | IDs, spans, `Diagnostic{code,severity,span,related,fix}`, units |
| `globe-mode-ir` | Frontend-neutral IR: `Outline`, `ConnectSet`, `DaeStructure` (variables + attributes, incidence, initial equations) |
| `globe-mode-frontend` | `trait Frontend`. Feature `rumoca` is the only place that imports Rumoca's front-end crates; feature `native` is Phase B |
| `globe-mode-db` | salsa 0.28 queries, revisions, cancellation |
| `globe-mode-analysis` | Hopcroft–Karp, Dulmage–Mendelsohn, Tarjan BLT, Pantelides, simulatability report. Works on neutral IR, so it carries over to Phase B |
| `globe-mode-graph` | Connect, library and incidence graphs as graphology JSON with incremental diffs; a Rust ForceAtlas2 for CLI SVG and the Rust UIs |
| `globe-mode-exec` | `HybridSystem` implementations: Rumoca interpreter/cranelift/wasm (feature `rumoca-exec`), FMU-ME adapter |
| `globe-mode-solver` | `trait Integrator`, solver-name registry, own fixed-step and explicit RK; features `diffsol` (default) and `sundials` |
| `globe-mode-sim` | Hybrid-system driver; `ResultStore` (CSV, MAT v4, Arrow IPC) |
| `globe-mode-fmi`, `globe-mode-fmu-runtime` | Import, master algorithms and packager; a cdylib that implements the FMI 2/3 C API |
| `globe-mode-protocol` | serde types + JSON Schema, generating TypeScript (specta) and Python type stubs |
| `globe-mode` | Public Rust API (facade): `Workspace{open,set_text,outline,check,graph,simulate,export_fmu}` |
| `globe-mode-lsp` | `lsp-types` plus our own dispatcher over a `Transport` (stdio, Tauri channel, Web Worker). No tokio |
| `globe-mode-cli` | Binary `globe` |
| `globe-mode-{capi,py,nif,wasm}` | C ABI + cbindgen header (Zig, Julia); PyO3 abi3-py312; Rustler NIF registered as Erlang module `globe_mode_nif` (Elixir and Gleam `@external` both use it); wasm-bindgen (browser, Gleam JS target, marimo anywidget) |
| `globe-mode-testkit`, `xtask` | MSL corpus runs, funnel comparison, omc oracle, Reference-FMUs; build/bump/license tasks |
| `apps/web`, `apps/desktop` | Svelte 5 app with a TypeScript `Backend` interface (`TauriBackend` or `WorkerBackend`); Tauri 2 shell for Linux, Windows and Android |
| `apps/experimental/{leptos,dioxus}` | Left out of `default-members` |

**Toolchain.** `rust-toolchain.toml` pins `1.99.1` with targets wasm32-unknown-unknown, wasm32-wasip2 and aarch64-linux-android.
- **Default build is stable.** Rumoca's nightly pin only applies inside Rumoca's own workspace. As a git dependency it builds with our toolchain.
- **Nightly is only for one optional artifact.** Threaded wasm needs `-Z build-std` and atomics, so it gets a separate nightly-only command: `xtask wasm --threads` runs `cargo +nightly-2026-02-27`. That build needs COOP/COEP headers or coi-serviceworker.
- **The default browser build is stable and single-threaded.** Rayon should fall back to running on the current thread; P1 must confirm this.
- **P1 checkpoint:** if `rumoca-compile` doesn't build on stable, try in order:
  1. an upstream PR to put the nightly-only code behind a `cfg`;
  2. a pinned fork via `[patch]`;
  3. moving the whole workspace onto the nightly pin, as a last resort.

**Keeping Rumoca contained.**
- All `rumoca-*` crates come from one `rev` with `default-features = false`. Never use the umbrella `rumoca` crate, because it pulls in zenoh.
- cargo-deny `bans` with `wrappers = ["globe-mode-frontend", "globe-mode-exec"]` block any other crate from importing Rumoca.
- Also ban `zenoh*` and `russell_sparse`.

## 2. Lazy evaluation
We use salsa from Phase A.
- **Inputs:** `SourceFile{path,text}` (user files at `Durability::LOW`, MSL at `HIGH`), `LibraryRoot`, `Settings`.
- **Query chain:**
  1. `parse(file)`: Rumoca AST in an `Arc`, marked `no_eq`.
  2. `outline(file)`: neutral IR with `Eq`. This is the main **firewall**: whitespace or comment edits produce the same outline, so nothing downstream reruns.
  3. `class_index(root)`: built from `package.order`. MSL files are only parsed when a name lookup needs them.
  4. `class_interface(path)`: components after resolving `extends`, plus flow/stream/potential variables.
  5. From there, two independent queries:
     - `connect_graph(path, depth)`: needs **no flatten**; nodes expand on demand.
     - `deps_closure(model)`: an over-approximated file set handed to Rumoca, so invalidation stays precise.
  6. `flat(model)`: Rumoca resolve and flatten, run under `catch_unwind`.
  7. `dae_structure(model)`: neutral IR with `Eq`, a second firewall.
  8. `simulatability(model)`.
  9. `executable(model, backend)`: kept in an LRU cache.
- **Simulation runs are jobs outside salsa.** Their results are cached by a hash of (executable, settings).

**Simulatability check** (`assert_simulatable`). It runs before every simulate and export, and refuses on any Error unless `--force` is given. It stops at the first stage that has errors, but reports every finding within that stage.
- **S0 parse.**
- **S1 resolve:** unresolved names, `uses` version mismatches.
- **S2 types:** type and dimension errors; unit mismatches are warnings.
- **S3 instantiate:** a `partial` top-level class, replaceables that were never redeclared.
  - Compiler limitations (stream, expandable or overconstrained connectors, state machines, operator records) get their own **GM-U** codes. That way "the compiler can't do this yet" is never confused with "your model is wrong".
- **S4 connections:** connector balance (MLS 9.3), incompatible connectors, unconnected connectors (warning), top-level inputs (info; these become FMU inputs).
- **S5 balance:** global count plus per-component local balance (MLS 4.7), so the failing component is named.
- **S6 structural singularity:** perfect matching; if there is none, Dulmage–Mendelsohn blocks show the over- and under-determined parts with source spans.
- **S7 index:** Pantelides and state selection.
- **S8 initialization:** balance of the initialization system, states without a start value, over-fixed variables, parameters with no value.
- **S9 algebraic loops:** nonlinear iteration variables without a start value.
- **S10 preflight:** parameter evaluation, residual at t0 is finite, chosen solver can handle the problem, experiment annotation is sane.

**Output.**
- `SimulatabilityReport{verdict, stages[], stats}` appears as LSP diagnostics, ariadne-style CLI output, protocol JSON, and highlights in the incidence graph.
- The S5 oracle is the equation and variable counts from `omc checkModel()`.

## 3. Solvers
```rust
trait HybridSystem {
  fn dims(&amp;self) -&gt; Dims; // n_x, n_roots, form: Ode|MassMatrix|ImplicitDae
  fn rhs(&amp;mut self, t: f64, x: &amp;[f64], dx: &amp;mut [f64]) -&gt; Eval;
  fn residual(&amp;mut self, t: f64, x: &amp;[f64], dx: &amp;[f64], r: &amp;mut [f64]) -&gt; Eval; // optional
  fn jacobian(&amp;mut self, t: f64, x: &amp;[f64], j: &amp;mut dyn JacSink) -&gt; Eval;      // optional, else colored FD
  fn roots(&amp;mut self, t: f64, x: &amp;[f64], g: &amp;mut [f64]) -&gt; Eval;
  fn next_time_event(&amp;self) -&gt; Option&lt;f64&gt;;
  fn event_update(&amp;mut self, t: f64, x: &amp;mut [f64]) -&gt; EventOutcome;
}
trait Integrator {
  fn caps(&amp;self) -&gt; Caps; // implicit, adaptive, dae, native_roots, dense, wasm
  fn init(&amp;mut self, s: &amp;mut dyn HybridSystem, t0: f64, x0: &amp;[f64], o: &amp;SolverOptions) -&gt; Result&lt;()&gt;;
  fn step(&amp;mut self, s: &amp;mut dyn HybridSystem, t_stop: f64) -&gt; Result&lt;Step&gt;; // Advanced|Root|Reached
  fn dense(&amp;self, t: f64, x: &amp;mut [f64]) -&gt; Result&lt;()&gt;;
  fn reset(&amp;mut self, t: f64, x: &amp;[f64]) -&gt; Result&lt;()&gt;;
}
```
- **The driver in `globe-mode-sim` owns the hybrid loop:** time events, finding roots (natively, or via dense output plus Illinois), event iteration, reset.
- **Modelica models and FMI-ME FMUs share that driver.**
- **Algebraic loops** use damped Newton on faer LU, or KINSOL when the `sundials` feature is on.

| ID | OpenModelica | Dymola | Backend (license, runs in wasm) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `bdf` (default) | dassl† | Dassl†, Lsodar† | diffsol BDF (MIT, yes) |
| `ida`, `cvode` | ida, cvode | Cvode | SUNDIALS (BSD-3, no) |
| `esdirk34`, `trbdf2` | gbode esdirk | Esdirk34a | diffsol SDIRK (MIT, yes) |
| `esdirk23/45`, `sdirk34hw` | gbode | Esdirk23a/45a, Sdirk34hw | diffsol with our own tableaux (yes) |
| `tsit45` | – | – | diffsol (yes) |
| `dopri45/853` | gbode dopri45 | Dopri45/853 | own explicit RK, checked against ode_solvers (yes) |
| `radau5` | gbode radau | Radau IIa | own, in P8. russell_ode is banned because it links GPL UMFPACK |
| `euler/heun/rk4/impeuler/trapezoid` | same names / rungekutta | Euler, Rkfix2/3/4 | own fixed-step (yes) |
| `cerk*`, `qss` | qss | Cerk23/34/45 | later |

† These are aliases. Picking one prints "dassl → bdf (same name, not bit-identical results)". KLU is LGPL and is never shipped.

## 4. FMI
**Import** (built on `fmi` 0.8 and `fmi-schema`):
- **ME:** `FmuMeSystem` implements `HybridSystem`, so it reuses the driver and every integrator.
- **CS:** `CoSimUnit{do_step, get/set, get/set_state, early_return}`.
- **SE (FMI 3):** `ScheduledUnit{activate_model_partition}` plus a priority-based clock scheduler.
- **Master algorithm:**
  - First: fixed-step Jacobi or Gauss–Seidel, with order taken from `ModelStructure` direct-feedthrough data; algebraic loops use fixed-point iteration.
  - Then: variable step with rollback when the FMU has `canGetAndSetFMUState`, FMI 3 event mode and early return, and SE.
  - The system topology comes from SSP or a `.gmsys` JSON file.
- **Platform limits:** native FMU binaries only load on desktop and Android. In the browser you can inspect `modelDescription.xml` and run FMUs that ship wasm binaries.

**Export:**
- **P5a (fast path):** Rumoca's `fmi2`/`fmi3` C templates produce source FMUs, and `cc` or `zig cc` builds the binaries for each target. We still need to check what license the generated template code carries.
- **P5b (own runtime):** `globe-mode-fmu-runtime`, built ahead of time per platform on the BSD-2 FMI headers, covering FMI 2 ME/CS and FMI 3 ME/CS/SE.
  - The model is either embedded as IR and interpreted, which needs no compiler and so also works from the browser and Android, or compiled through Rumoca's `rust-ode` template.
  - CS FMUs step with our own integrators.
  - `ModelStructure` comes from the BLT incidence.
  - `fmi-export` is only an option for FMI 3. FMI 2 export has no existing crate and must be written ourselves.

**Validation:**
- Reference-FMUs (BSD-2) for FMI 2 and 3 — BouncingBall, Dahlquist, VanDerPol, Stair, Feedthrough, Resource, Clocks — compared against their reference CSVs.
- Our exported FMUs must pass `fmpy.validate_fmu`, simulate in FMPy and in `fmusim`, and pass the FMU Compliance Checker (FMI 2 only).
- Round trip: export, re-import, then compare with a direct simulation using funnel tolerances.
- OMSimulator only as a subprocess.

## 5. Roadmap

| Phase | Scope | Exit criteria | Sessions |
|---|---|---|---|
| P0 Ops (S001) | Docs, rules, hooks, skills | Hook writes `log/sessions/`; slide deck and 解説書 exist; ADRs 0001–0005 written | 1 |
| P1 Skeleton | Workspace, toolchain, deny, CI (Linux, Windows, wasm), core/ir/frontend/cli, MSL fetch script (`data/msl`, sha256) | `cargo deny check` passes; `globe check` matches Rumoca on 20 MSL examples; frontend builds for wasm32; ADR-0003 decided | 3: workspace + CI, adapter + CLI, wasm checkpoint |
| P2 Lazy queries + checks | db, outline, index, connect graph, S0–S10 | Re-check after a whitespace edit takes &lt;50 ms and `flat` doesn't rerun (per salsa events); every MSL example gets a verdict with 0 panics; S5 matches omc on ≥95% of flattenable models | 4 |
| P3 Simulation | exec, driver, bdf/esdirk34/tsit45/dopri/fixed-step, ResultStore | ≥153 MSL examples simulate; ≥90% within funnel of the MSL reference results; bdf runs as wasm in Node | 5 |
| P4 Desktop MVP | CodeMirror 6 + LSP, library tree, check panel, uPlot, sigma.js + ForceAtlas2 | ChuaCircuit: open, check, simulate, plot; 5k-node graph at ≥30 fps; memory (RSS) baseline recorded; CI builds .deb and .msi | 6 |
| P5 FMI | Import ME/CS/SE + master; export P5a then P5b | All Reference-FMUs match on linux64 and win64; exports pass FMPy, fmusim and fmuCheck; Clocks example (SE) round-trips | 8 |
| P6 Web/marimo/Android | WorkerBackend, MSL bundles loaded on demand, anywidget, PyO3 wheel, APK | Browser simulates under CSP `connect-src 'self'` (no server compute); marimo WASM notebook stays under 2 GB; Android API 34 emulator runs check and simulate | 5 |
| P7 Language APIs + UI experiments | C/Zig/Julia, Elixir/Gleam, Python; Leptos and Dioxus variants plus a benchmark harness (startup, RSS/PSS, CPU, frame time, bundle size) | One reference scenario passes in all 6 languages; `doc/bench/ui.md` compares the 3 UIs on 3 OSes | 6 |
| P8 Breadth | radau5, SUNDIALS, ESDIRK/CERK, SSP 2.0.1, diagram view (Svelte Flow + Placement annotations), BLT debugger | Solver table complete; SSP round trip | open |
| Track B (own compiler core) | B1 rowan CST and error-tolerant parser, B2 resolve, B3 flatten, B4 DAE, as the `native` `Frontend` | Native becomes the default once it matches or beats Rumoca at each stage (parse 566 / flatten 562 / balanced 464 / simulate 153) with 0 regressions in `xtask diff-frontend` | Starts after P4, when resources allow |

Track B1–B2 can drive IDE features while compilation stays on Rumoca. Because the two ASTs are different, the only place the frontends can be mixed is that split between IDE features and the compile pipeline.

## 6. Risks

| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| Rumoca's nightly pin | Stable workspace with a nightly-only threaded-wasm build; P1 checkpoint; fork as fallback |
| Rumoca API churn and missing features | One pinned `rev`; cargo-deny `wrappers`; neutral IR firewalls; monthly `xtask bump-rumoca` with a corpus diff; GM-U codes; upstream PRs |
| wasm limits (no JIT, no dlopen, no threads by default; 4 GB memory, 2 GB in marimo; no SUNDIALS) | Interpreter, or Rumoca wasm codegen loaded via `WebAssembly.instantiate`; diffsol as default; MSL compressed per package |
| Android WebView performance | Compute natively in-process; cranelift off; graph level-of-detail and node caps; ForceAtlas2 iteration budget in a worker; performance budgets on a real device |
| License drift (Rust, npm, generated code) | cargo-deny allowlist (MIT, Apache, BSD, ISC, Zlib, Unicode, CC0); `pnpm licenses` check in CI; cargo-about NOTICES; CycloneDX SBOM; omc only as a subprocess |
| FMI 2 export with no crate to build on | The three validators above plus round trips |
| Scope (6 languages × 5 targets) | One API facade and one protocol crate; CI split into tiers |
| Raw transcripts in a public repo (they contain the user's email address and local paths) | gitignore `log/sessions/raw/`, or redact before pushing |

## 7. Session-001 deliverables (docs and ops only)
- `CLAUDE.md` in Japanese:
  - worktree flow: `feature/&lt;topic&gt;` in `.git-worktree/&lt;topic&gt;`, `uv sync` in each worktree, never share `.venv`;
  - a table of what each directory is for;
  - license rules, and "Rumoca only through the adapter crates";
  - the duty to write session docs, and a finish checklist.
- `README.md`, `LICENSE-MIT`, `LICENSE-APACHE`.
- `doc/architecture.md`, `doc/roadmap.md`, `doc/licenses.md`.
- `doc/adr/0001-license-policy.md`, `0002-rumoca-adapter.md`, `0003-toolchain.md` (status "proposed" until the P1 checkpoint), `0004-ui-tauri-svelte.md`, `0005-lazy-query-db.md`.
- `doc/research/{rumoca,omc-dymola,standards,fmi-rust,solvers,incremental,ui-graph,bindings}.md`. Every fact gets a URL and "verified 2026-10-18".
- `doc/sessions/README.md`: an index mapping S-numbers to Claude session IDs and branches.
- `doc/sessions/S001-research-plan/{slides.html,解説書.md}`.
- Shared `doc/assets/slides.{css,js}`: works offline, uses CSS variables for light and dark, keyboard navigation, no CDN, no per-deck `&lt;style&gt;`.
- `log/README.md` and `log/work/{_TEMPLATE.md,2026-10-18-research-plan.md}`.
- `.claude/hooks/save_session_log.py`, copied from the sibling repo with `SESSIONS = ROOT / "log" / "sessions"`.
  - In a worktree, `ROOT` is the worktree, so logs are written there and committed with the feature branch.
  - Removing the worktree before committing loses them.
- `.claude/settings.json`: Stop (async) and SessionEnd hooks as in the sibling, plus permissions for read-only cargo, git and uv commands.
- Three skills under `.claude/skills/`:
  - `start-session`: create the worktree, the `doc/sessions/SNNN-&lt;topic&gt;/` skeleton and the work-log draft.
  - `finish-session`: fmt, clippy, test and deny once code exists, then session-docs, then the work log, then a commit to the feature branch.
  - `session-docs`: the sections for the deck and 解説書 — 目的, 決定と理由, 図, 再現手順, 次回.
- `.gitignore` additions: `.git-worktree/`, the sibling's `.claude/` local-state block, `node_modules/`, `data/msl/`, and a decision on `log/sessions/raw/`.
- Optional ops fix: rename the Python package to `globe_mode`.

  - Rumoca 本体は Apache-2.0 で、NOTICE ファイルはありません。
  - 配布物には依存ライブラリのライセンス表示を同梱する必要があります。
  - 製品名に「Rumoca」は使えません。
  - umbrella crate `rumoca` は zenoh などを引き込むため使わず、`rumoca-compile` などの個別 crate に絞ります。
- **ライセンスより大きいリスク**: Rumoca が nightly ツールチェーンに固定されていることです。stable でビルドできるかは S002（P1）で確かめます。
- **アーキテクチャ**:
  - **遅延評価**: salsa のクエリで、空白やコメントだけの編集では下流を再計算しません。connect グラフは flatten しなくても描けます。
  - **シミュレーション前の検査**: S0〜S10 の11段階で検査し、Error があれば実行を止めます。
  - **ソルバ**: OpenModelica や Dymola の名前で指定でき、内部の実装に対応づけます。
  - **FMI**: 2.0 と 3.0 の Import／Export を、Reference-FMUs と FMPy で検証します。
- **配置**: ユーザーが作った `src/<言語>/` の構成に合わせ、`src/Zig/` と `src/Svelte/` を追加します。
- **ロードマップ**: P0（今回）から P8 までと、自前コアに移る Track B です。
- **Python パッケージ名**: `20261018_globemode` は数字で始まるため import できません。P1 で `globe_mode` に改名します。
- **セッション記録**: 伏せ字にした整形版だけをコミットし、生ログは git の管理外に置きます。
  - このセッションでは main にまだフックが無いため、記録は手動で書き出します。
- **スライドの確認**: この環境にはブラウザも node も無いので、見た目は `file://` で開いて確認してください。

  - [licenses.md](.git-worktree/s001-research-plan/doc/licenses.md)、設計判断の記録（ADR）5本

- **運用基盤**:
  - [CLAUDE.md](.git-worktree/s001-research-plan/CLAUDE.md)

5. 言語別の使い方
  mise は PATH にツールを出すだけなので、日常の操作は各言語の標準コマンドのままです。
  Python (uv)
  uv が .python-version を読み、必要なら CPython 3.12 を取得します。
    shellコピー
    uv sync
uv run 20261018-globemode
uv run python -V
  Rust (Cargo)
  ルートの Cargo.toml と src/main.rs をそのまま Cargo で扱います。rust-toolchain.toml は作らず、バージョンは mise.toml に一本化してください (mise が設定する RUSTUP_TOOLCHAIN が優先され、ファイルが無視されるため)。
    shellコピー
    cargo build
cargo run
cargo fmt --check
cargo clippy -- -D warnings
  Zig
    shellコピー
    cd src/Zig
zig init
zig build run
  Julia
  src/Julia に Project.toml ができた後の操作です。
    shellコピー
    julia --project=src/Julia -e 'using Pkg; Pkg.instantiate()'
julia --project=src/Julia
  Elixir
  初回に Hex と rebar を入れておくと、対話の確認で止まりません。
    shellコピー
    mix local.hex --force
mix local.rebar --force
cd src/Elixir
mix new . --app gl

//! Unified compilation session management for Rumoca.
//!
//! This crate provides a standardized interface for compiling Modelica code
//! across different frontends: CLI, LSP, WASM, etc.
//!
//! # Features
//!
//! - **Session management**: Track open documents and compilation state
//! - **Multi-file support**: Combine multiple files with within clause handling
//! - **Parallel compilation**: Compile multiple models concurrently
//! - **Incremental updates**: Update single documents without full recompilation

    pub fn compile_model(&mut self, model_name: &str) -> Result<CompilationResult> {
        match self.compile_model_phases(model_name)? {
            PhaseResult::Success(result) => Ok(*result),
            PhaseResult::NeedsInner { missing_inners, .. } => Err(anyhow::anyhow!(
                "Instantiate error: missing inner declarations: {}",
                missing_inners.join(", ")
            )),
            PhaseResult::Failed {
                phase,
                error,
                error_code,
                ..

**S002（P1 の 1 回目）** で行うこと:

1. ルートの `Cargo.toml` を virtual workspace にする。`src/main.rs` を `src/Rust/mod/globe-mode-cli` に移す。
2. `mise.toml` の rust に wasm と Android の `targets` を足す。`rust-toolchain.toml` は置かない（ツールの版は mise、依存は uv と cargo）。

## 🤖 Claude　`2026-10-18 02:28:46`

S001 を main に取り込み、続けて S002（P1 骨格の1回目）まで進めて、main にマージしました（main の先頭は `e6788d1`）。最大の懸念だった「Rumoca は stable の Rust でビルドできるか」は、ネイティブでも wasm32 でもビルドが通り、解消しました。

**S001 の取り込み**
- main のコミット `db789c4`（mise の導入）を worktree に取り込んでから、main にマージしました。
- そのコミットには worktree のディレクトリがサブモジュール扱いの参照（gitlink）として入っていました。クローンすると壊れたサブモジュールになるので、index から外しました。
- 衝突した `.claude/settings.json` と `.gitignore` は、main の内容をすでに含んでいる S001 側の版を採りました。

**言語管理を mise・uv・cargo に統一**
- ツールの版は mise、Python の依存は uv、Rust の依存は cargo で管理します。ADR-0003 などの文書もこの方針に直しました。
- `rust-toolchain.toml` は置きません（手順書と同じ方針）。
- Playwright は uv の開発依存、cargo-deny は mise で入れました。
- 検査は `mise run check` と `mise run docs:check` の2つにまとめました。

**Playwright での確認**
- 全デッキを、ライト・ダーク・スマホの3つの表示で開いて検査するスクリプトを作りました。
- 数値の検査では問題0件でしたが、スクリーンショットを見て5件の表示崩れを見つけ、直しました。
  - ページ番号が操作ボタンに隠れていた。
  - スマホで図が縮みすぎて読めなかった。
  - 図の文字のはみ出しやラベルの重なりが3か所あった。
- 現在は3デッキとも問題0件です。

**S002 の結果**
- **Rumoca**: v0.10.2 の `rumoca-compile` が、nightly なしの stable 1.99.1 でビルドできました。ネイティブでも wasm32 でも通ります。
- **CLI `globe`**: crate を4つ（core・ir・frontend・cli）作りました。Rumoca を呼べるのはアダプタの1ファイルだけです。
  - 自作の RC 回路は「方程式20本 = 変数20個」で検査を通過します。
  - 方程式が足りない例は S5、構文誤りの例は S0 で止まり、終了コード 1 を返します。
- **ライセンス監査（cargo-deny）**: 4項目とも ok です。
  - アダプタ以外から Rumoca を直接使うと検出されることも、わざと違反させて確かめました。
  - Rumoca 経由で入る保守終了の勧告2件（脆弱性ではない）は、理由を書いて例外にしました。
- **テストと静的解析**: テスト10件がすべて通り、clippy の警告は0件です。
- **Python**: パッケージを `src/Python/mod/globe_mode` に改名し、`uv run globe-mode` で動くことを確かめました。

**確認してほしいこと**
- **CI は未実行**です。`.github/workflows/ci.yml` は push していないので、GitHub 上では一度も動いていません。push するかはお任せします。
- **`uv_build` の範囲を変えました。** `pyproject.toml` で `>=0.13.1,<0.14.0` にしています。元の `<0.13.1` は mise の uv 0.13.1 を含まず、ビルドのたびに uv_build を PyPI から取り直していたためです。意図と違えば戻してください。
- **main で `uv sync` を実行してください。** パッケージ名を変えたため、main の `.venv` には旧名のインストールが残っています。
- **main の未追跡の `data/.gitkeep`** には触れていません。
- **個人のメールアドレス**が、git の作者情報と `pyproject.toml` の authors にあります。公開前に GitHub の noreply アドレスに変えるか検討してください。
- **worktree の削除**: `.git-worktree/s001-*` と `s002-*` は残しています。消すかはお任せします。

次の S003 では、MSL 4.1.0 を取得し、その例題20本で `globe check` の結果を Rumoca と突き合わせます。

## 🤖 Claude　`2026-10-18 02:46:38`

GitHub Actions は、`.github/workflows/ci.yml` を含むコミットを GitHub に push すると自動で動きます。このワークフローが起動するのは次の2つのときです。

- `main` への push
- Pull Request の作成・更新

いまのローカルの main は、`origin/main`（`db789c4`）より9コミット進んでいて、CI の設定はまだ GitHub にありません。

## 方法1: main をそのまま push する

```bash
cd ~/monorepo/Chaos/20261018_GlobeMode
git push origin main
```

push したら、`https://github.com/asari300/20261018_GlobeMode/actions` を開くと「ci」ワークフローが動いています。

## 方法2: PR で試す（main を汚さない）

1. 確認用のブランチを push します。

   ```bash
   git push origin feature/s002-workspace-skeleton
   ```

2. GitHub の画面に出る「Compare & pull request」から PR を作ると、CI が動きます。この環境には `gh` CLI が無いので、PR は Web UI で作ってください。
3. CI が通ったら、その PR をマージします。

CI がまだ一度も動いていない状態なので、こちらをおすすめします。

## push の前に確認してほしいこと

| 項目 | 内容 |
| --- | --- |
| Actions が有効か | リポジトリの Settings → Actions → General で「Allow all actions」（またはこのワークフローが使う `actions/*`、`jdx/mise-action`、`Swatinem/rust-cache` を許可）になっていること |
| 実行時間 | Linux 2ジョブと Windows 1ジョブ。初回はキャッシュが無いので、Rumoca のビルドに1ジョブあたり数分かかる見込みです。非公開リポジトリでは無料枠の分数を消費し、Windows は2倍で数えられます |
| メールアドレス | リポジトリが公開なら、コミットの作者欄の Gmail アドレスも公開されます（既存のコミットも同じ） |

## 初回に失敗しうる箇所

一度も実行していないので、次の点で止まる可能性があります。ログを見せてもらえれば直します。

- **mise-action**: `mise.lock` には Linux と Windows の分も記録されているはずですが、確かめていません。
- **Windows ジョブ**: ローカルで試していません。
- **Playwright の `--with-deps`**: apt でブラウザの依存を入れるため、少し時間がかかります。

あわせて、ワークフローに `workflow_dispatch` を足しておくと、Actions の画面の「Run workflow」ボタンから手動でも実行できます。追加と push をこちらで行う場合は、方法1と方法2のどちらにするか指示してください。

## 🤖 Claude　`2026-10-18 02:58:20`

`feature/ops-ci-push-main` を main にマージしました（`c514fef`）。いまの main は origin/main より2コミット進んでいて、push はしていません。

**worktree の掃除はまだしていません。** リポジトリが非公開のため、GitHub の API から CI の結果を読めず、「CI が通ったら」の条件を確かめられないからです。Actions の画面（`https://github.com/asari300/20261018_GlobeMode/actions`）で、`c22e1e2` の ci が緑かどうかを教えてください。

通っていれば、次の分を片付けます。

| worktree | ブランチ | 扱い |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `s001-research-plan` | `feature/s001-research-plan` | **削除**（2つ前で、main に取り込み済み） |
| `s002-workspace-skeleton` | `feature/s002-workspace-skeleton` | 残す（1つ前） |
| `ops-ci-push-main` | `feature/ops-ci-push-main` | 残す（最新） |

「2つ以上前」は「最新から数えて2つ以上前」と解釈しました。削除はディレクトリの削除（`git worktree remove`）とブランチの削除（`git branch -d`）の両方です。`-d` は取り込み済みのブランチしか消さない、安全な削除です。範囲が違えば指示してください。

今後 CI の結果をこちらで確かめるには、次のどちらかが必要です。

- `gh` CLI を入れて認証する（例: `brew install gh && gh auth login`）
- Actions を読み取れる権限の GitHub トークンを使う（そのときは扱い方を相談させてください）

## 👤 ユーザー　`2026-10-18 03:00:58`

CIは更新部分のみとしたい。依存関係を検証済みの部分はスキップし、大規模な変更時のみ全体テストで効率化することは可能か。可能ならその旨を更新し、マージまで進める

## 🤖 Claude　`2026-10-18 03:01:19`

<details><summary>思考</summary>



</details>

## 🤖 Claude　`2026-10-18 03:01:21`

可能です。GitHub Actions では、次の組み合わせで「変更した部分だけ検証し、大きな変更のときだけ全体を回す」形にできます。

- 変更されたファイルから、検査の範囲を決める。
- crate の依存関係から、影響を受ける crate だけをテストする。
- 依存を変えていなければ、依存の監査を省く。
- 大きな変更や週1回の定期実行では、全体を回す。

worktree を切って実装します。

## 🤖 Claude　`2026-10-18 03:04:52`

ご要望の差分 CI は実装でき、main へのマージまで済ませました（`c38639a`、push はしていません）。GitHub 上ではまだ一度も動かしていません。

**新しい CI の動き**

最初に判定用のジョブ（plan）が、変更されたファイルから「何を検査するか」を決めます。

| 変更の例 | 実行されるもの |
| --- | --- |
| 文書だけ | 文書のリンク検査だけ |
| `globe-mode-cli` のソースだけ | cli だけを clippy と test |
| `globe-mode-ir` のソース | ir と、ir に依存する frontend・cli、wasm32 のビルド確認 |
| `Cargo.lock` などの依存 | 全体（Windows と依存のライセンス監査を含む） |

- **検証済みの依存は省きます。** 依存のライセンス監査（`cargo deny`）は、`Cargo.lock`・`Cargo.toml`・`deny.toml` を変えたときだけ動きます。コンパイル済みの依存はキャッシュを使い回すので、`Cargo.lock` が同じなら再ビルドしません。
- **全体を検査するのは次のときだけです。**
  - 依存・ツール・CI の定義を変えたとき
  - 変更が40ファイルを超えたとき
  - コミットメッセージに `[full-ci]` があるとき
  - 毎週月曜 03:00 JST の定期実行
  - Actions 画面からの手動実行
- **Windows のジョブ**は、全体を検査するときだけ動きます。時間がかかり、非公開リポジトリの無料枠では2倍で数えられるためです。
- **push の前に `mise run ci:plan` を実行すると**、その push で何が検査されるかを手元で確認できます。

**確かめたこと**
- 判定ロジックの単体テスト12件はすべて通りました。
- 過去のコミットで判定させた結果は期待どおりでした。
  - 文書だけの変更では文書の検査だけになる。
  - S001（47ファイル）は全体の検査になる。
- 4種類の起動方法（push・新しいブランチ・定期実行・手動実行）を手元で模擬し、期待どおりの判定になりました。
- GitHub Actions 用の検査ツール（actionlint）の指摘は0件でした。

ADR-0006 を新しく書き、CLAUDE.md・締めのスキル・S002 の解説書とスライド（S12）にも反映しました。

**次の push について**
- 今回は CI の定義そのものを変えたので、次の push は全体の検査になります。
- 想定外の点が1つあります。コミットメッセージの説明文に `[full-ci]` という文字列を書いたため、それにも反応しました。今回はもともと全体の検査になるので影響はありません。今後は説明でこの文字列を書かないようにします。

**保留していること: worktree の掃除**
- リポジトリが非公開のため、こちらからは CI の結果を読めず、「CI が通ったら」の条件を確かめられていません。
- Actions の画面で緑だったかを教えていただければ、`s001-research-plan` の worktree とブランチを削除します。
- 「2つ以上前」は「最新から数えて2つ以上前」と解釈しています。残す worktree は `s002`・`ops-ci-push-main`・`ops-ci-incremental` の3つで、新しい1本が増えています。範囲が違えば指示してください。

## 🤖 Claude　`2026-10-18 03:12:26`

失敗した run（`c22e1e2`）は、Windows のジョブの `cargo test` だけが落ちていました。修正して main にマージ済みです（`f08bb5d`、push はしていません）。

## 失敗の原因と修正

| ジョブ | 結果 |
| --- | --- |
| rust-linux | 成功（fmt、clippy、test、deny、wasm32） |
| python-and-docs | 成功（Playwright を含む） |
| **rust-windows** | **`cargo test` で失敗** |

注釈を読むと、Windows では `cargo` を呼んだ時点で、mise が指定外のツール7つ（zig・julia・erlang など）を自動で入れ始め、そのあと `rustc -vV` で止まっていました。Linux では同じ設定で通っています。ログの全文は公開リポジトリでも認証が要る（403）ため読めず、原因は注釈から判断しました。

直したこと:
- **Windows のジョブ**: mise を通さず、ランナーに入っている rustup の stable を使うようにしました。`mise.lock` の rust も stable なので版はそろいます。ADR-0003 に例外として書きました。
- **全ジョブ**: `MISE_AUTO_INSTALL=false` にして、実行中にツールが勝手に入らないようにしました。
- **Actions の版**: 「Node.js 20 は非推奨」の警告が出ていたので、checkout と upload-artifact を v7、mise-action を v5 に上げました。

次の push は CI の定義を変えているので全体の検査になり、Windows のジョブも動きます。push してもらえれば、こちらで結果を確認します。前の run が失敗なので、worktree の掃除はその結果が出るまで保留しています。

## リポジトリを非公開にしても CI の結果を見る方法

認証すれば読めます。ログの全文は公開リポジトリでも認証が要るので、いずれにしても用意しておくと原因を調べやすくなります。

**おすすめ: `gh` CLI**

```bash
brew install gh
gh auth login          # ブラウザで認証する
```

導入すれば、私が `gh run list`・`gh run view <id> --log-failed` で結果と失敗したログを直接読めます。

**代わりの方法: 権限を絞ったトークン（Fine-grained personal access token）**
- GitHub の Settings → Developer settings → Fine-grained tokens で作ります。
  - 対象リポジトリ: `20261018_GlobeMode` だけ
  - 権限: Actions を Read-only、Metadata を Read-only（自動で付く）
  - 有効期限: 短めに
- トークンはチャットに貼らないでください。ご自身の端末で、たとえば `~/.config/globe-mode/gh-token`（権限 600）に保存し、場所だけ教えてもらえれば、そこから読んで API に渡す形にします。リポジトリには入れません。

どちらかを用意してもらえれば、リポジトリを非公開に戻しても今と同じように確認できます。

「2つ以上前」は「最新から数えて2つ以上前」と解釈し、3つの worktree とそのブランチを削除しました。いずれも未コミットの変更が無く、main に取り込み済みであることを確かめてから消しています。

- 残した2つ: `ops-ci-incremental`（1つ前）、`ops-ci-windows-fix`（最新）

今回は CI の定義を変えた push だったので全体を検査しました。次からの通常の push では変更部分だけを検査するので、rust-linux の約10分もかなり短くなる見込みです。

「CI が通ったら2つ以上前の worktree を掃除する」を毎回の締めの手順として CLAUDE.md と finish-session スキルに入れることもできます。入れる場合は指示してください。

## 🤖 Claude　`2026-10-18 03:45:15`

<details><summary>思考</summary>

原因が分かりました。`model_names()` がMSL解析時の警告（WR006・WR001）までErrとして拾ってしまい、それをS1のエラーにしていたためです。model_names()失敗時は処理を止めずコンパイルを試し、ライブラリ指定時はモデル名の事前確認を省くよう直します。なお48秒かかったのはデバッグビルドのせいで、比較はリリースビルドで行います。

</details>
